```
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    19→from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    20→from desloppify.state import path_scoped_findings
    21→
    22→
    23→def enrich_with_impact(items: list[dict], dimension_scores: dict) -> None:
    24→    """Stamp ``estimated_impact`` on each item based on dimension-level headroom.
    25→
    26→    Impact = ``overall_per_point * headroom`` where headroom = ``100 - score``.
    27→    Items in dimensions with more score headroom sort first.
    28→    """
    29→    if not dimension_scores:
    30→        for item in items:
    31→            item["estimated_impact"] = 0.0
    32→        return
    33→
    34→    from desloppify.engine._scoring.results.health import compute_health_breakdown
    35→    from desloppify.engine._scoring.results.impact import get_dimension_for_detector
    36→
    37→    breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dimension_scores)
    38→    entries = breakdown.get("entries", [])
    39→
    40→    # Build lookup: normalized dimension name -> {per_point, headroom}
    41→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    42→    for entry in entries:
    43→        name = str(entry.get("name", "")).strip()
    44→        if not name:
    45→            continue
    46→        per_point = float(entry.get("overall_per_point", 0.0))
    47→        score = float(entry.get("score", 0.0))
    48→        headroom = 100.0 - score
    49→        dim_impact[name.lower()] = {"per_point": per_point, "headroom": headroom}
    50→
    51→    for item in items:
    52→        impact = _compute_item_impact(item, dim_impact, get_dimension_for_detector)
    53→        item["estimated_impact"] = impact
    54→
    55→
    56→def _compute_item_impact(
    57→    item: dict,
    58→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]],
    59→    get_dimension_for_detector,
    60→) -> float:
    61→    """Compute impact value for a single queue item."""
    62→    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
    63→
    64→    # Subjective items (synthetic dimensions or subjective findings):
    65→    # look up by detail.dimension_name
    66→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
    67→        dim_name = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    68→        entry = dim_impact.get(dim_name.lower())
    69→        if entry:
    70→            return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
    71→        return 0.0
    72→
    73→    # Mechanical findings: use detector -> dimension mapping
    74→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    75→    if detector:
    76→        dimension = get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
    77→        if dimension:
    78→            entry = dim_impact.get(dimension.name.lower())
    79→            if entry:
    80→                return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
    81→
    82→    return 0.0
    83→
    84→
    85→def subjective_score_value(item: dict) -> float:
    86→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    87→        detail = detail_dict(item)
    88→        return float(detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0)))
    89→    return float(item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_finding_items(
    93→    state: dict,
    94→    *,
    95→    scan_path: str | None,
    96→    status_filter: str,
    97→    scope: str | None,
    98→    chronic: bool,
    99→) -> list[dict]:
   100→    scoped = path_scoped_findings(state.get("findings", {}), scan_path)
   101→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
   102→    out: list[dict] = []
   103→
   104→    for finding_id, finding in scoped.items():
   105→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
   106→            continue
   107→        if not status_matches(finding.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
   108→            continue
   109→        if chronic and not (
   110→            finding.get("status") == "open" and finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
   111→        ):
   112→            continue
   113→
   114→        # Evidence-only: skip findings below standalone confidence threshold
   115→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
   116→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   117→        if meta and meta.standalone_threshold:
   118→            threshold_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(meta.standalone_threshold, 9)
   119→            finding_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(finding.get("confidence", "low"), 9)
   120→            if finding_rank > threshold_rank:
   121→                continue
   122→
   123→        item = dict(finding)
   124→        item["id"] = finding_id
   125→        item["kind"] = "finding"
   126→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
   127→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
   128→        item["review_weight"] = (
   129→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
   130→        )
   131→        subjective_score = None
   132→        if item["is_subjective"]:
   133→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
   134→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
   135→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
   136→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
   137→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
   138→            )
   139→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
   140→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
   141→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
   142→            item,
   143→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
   144→        )
   145→
   146→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
   147→            continue
   148→        out.append(item)
   149→
   150→    return out
   151→
   152→
   153→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
   154→    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   155→
   156→    # Initial-review subjective items: highest priority (tier -3)
   157→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" and item.get("initial_review"):
   158→        return (-3, 0, subjective_score_value(item), item.get("id", ""))
   159→
   160→    # Triage stage items: tier -2, stage order, blocked after unblocked
   161→    if kind == "workflow_stage":
   162→        blocked_penalty = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
   163→        stage_index = int(item.get("stage_index", 0))
   164→        return (-2, blocked_penalty, stage_index, item.get("id", ""))
   165→
   166→    # Workflow action items (e.g. create-plan): tier -1
   167→    if kind == "workflow_action":
   168→        return (-1, 0, 0, item.get("id", ""))
   169→
   170→    if kind == "cluster":
   171→        # Clusters sort before individual findings, ordered by action type
   172→        action_pri = ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY.get(
   173→            item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"), 3
   174→        )
   175→        return (
   176→            0,
   177→            action_pri,
   178→            -int(item.get("member_count", 0)),
   179→            item.get("id", ""),
   180→        )
   181→
   182→    impact = item.get("estimated_impact", 0.0)
   183→
   184→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
   185→        return (
   186→            1,
   187→            -impact,
   188→            subjective_score_value(item),
   189→            item.get("id", ""),
   190→        )
   191→
   192→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   193→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   194→    return (
   195→        1,
   196→        -impact,
   197→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
   198→        -review_weight,
   199→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   200→        item.get("id", ""),
   201→    )
   202→
   203→
   204→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   205→    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   206→    if kind == "workflow_stage":
   207→        return {
   208→            "kind": "workflow_stage",
   209→            "stage": item.get("stage_name"),
   210→            "is_blocked": item.get("is_blocked", False),
   211→            "blocked_by": item.get("blocked_by", []),
   212→            "policy": "Triage stages sort by dependency order; blocked stages follow unblocked.",
   213→            "ranking_factors": ["blocked_penalty asc", "stage_index asc"],
   214→        }
   215→
   216→    if kind == "workflow_action":
   217→        return {
   218→            "kind": "workflow_action",
   219→            "policy": "Workflow items sort after triage stages, before findings.",
   220→            "ranking_factors": ["id asc"],
   221→        }
   222→
   223→    if kind == "cluster":
   224→        return {
   225→            "kind": "cluster",
   226→            "estimated_impact": item.get("estimated_impact", 0.0),
   227→            "action_type": item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"),
   228→            "member_count": item.get("member_count", 0),
   229→            "policy": "Clusters sort before individual findings, ordered by action type then size.",
   230→            "ranking_factors": ["action_type asc", "member_count desc", "id asc"],
   231→        }
   232→
   233→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
   234→        initial = item.get("initial_review", False)
   235→        return {
   236→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   237→            "estimated_impact": item.get("estimated_impact", 0.0),
   238→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   239→            "initial_review": initial,
   240→            "policy": (
   241→                "Initial review items sort first (onboarding priority)."
   242→                if initial else
   243→                "Sorted by dimension impact (score headroom × weight), then subjective score."
   244→            ),
   245→            "ranking_factors": ["estimated_impact desc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   246→        }
   247→
   248→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   249→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   250→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   251→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   252→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   253→    if is_subjective:
   254→        ranking_factors = ["estimated_impact desc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   255→    elif is_review:
   256→        ranking_factors = [
   257→            "estimated_impact desc",
   258→            "confidence asc",
   259→            "review_weight desc",
   260→            "count desc",
   261→            "id asc",
   262→        ]
   263→    else:
   264→        ranking_factors = ["estimated_impact desc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   265→    explain = {
   266→        "kind": "finding",
   267→        "estimated_impact": item.get("estimated_impact", 0.0),
   268→        "confidence": confidence,
   269→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   270→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   271→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   272→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   273→    }
   274→    if is_review:
   275→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   276→    if is_subjective:
   277→        explain["policy"] = (
   278→            "Sorted by dimension impact (score headroom × weight), then subjective score."
   279→        )
   280→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   281→    return explain
   282→
   283→
   284→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   285→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   286→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   287→    for item in items:
   288→        if group == "file":
   289→            key = item.get("file", "")
   290→        elif group == "detector":
   291→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   292→        elif group == "cluster":
   293→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   294→            key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
   295→        else:
   296→            key = "items"
   297→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   298→    return grouped
   299→
   300→

   302→    "build_finding_items",
   303→    "enrich_with_impact",
   304→    "item_explain",
   305→    "item_sort_key",
   306→    "subjective_score_value",
   307→    "group_queue_items",
```

```
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan, state_path

    38→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    39→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.STATE_FILE
    40→
    41→
    42→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    43→    return path if path is not None else PLAN_FILE
    44→
    45→
    46→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    47→    if state_file is None:
    48→        return None
    49→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    50→
    51→
    52→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    53→    if not path.exists():
    54→        return None
    55→    return path.read_text()
    56→
    57→
    58→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    59→    if snapshot is None:
    60→        try:
    61→            path.unlink()
    62→        except FileNotFoundError:
    63→            return
    64→        return
    65→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    66→
    67→
    68→def _save_plan_state_transactional(
    69→    *,
    70→    plan: dict,
    71→    plan_path: Path | None,
    72→    state_data: dict,
    73→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    74→) -> None:
    75→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    76→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    77→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    78→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    79→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    80→
    81→    try:
    82→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    83→        save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    84→    except Exception:
    85→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    86→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    87→        raise
    88→

    90→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    91→    """Set augmented description on findings."""
    92→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    93→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    94→        return
    95→
    96→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    97→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    98→
    99→    plan = load_plan()
   100→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   101→    if not finding_ids:
   102→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   103→        return
   104→
   105→    for fid in finding_ids:
   106→        describe_finding(plan, fid, text or None)
   107→    append_log_entry(
   108→        plan, "describe", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
   109→        detail={"text": text or None},
   110→    )
   111→    save_plan(plan)
   112→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
   113→
   114→
   115→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   116→    """Set note on findings."""
   117→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   118→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   119→        return
   120→
   121→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   122→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
   123→
   124→    plan = load_plan()
   125→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   126→    if not finding_ids:
   127→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   128→        return
   129→
   130→    for fid in finding_ids:
   131→        annotate_finding(plan, fid, text)
   132→    append_log_entry(
   133→        plan, "note", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
   134→        note=text,
   135→    )
   136→    save_plan(plan)
   137→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
   138→
   139→
   140→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   141→# Skip / unskip
   142→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   143→
   144→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   145→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
   146→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   147→    state = runtime.state
   148→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   149→        return
   150→
```

```
1→"""Plan persistence — load/save with atomic writes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    12→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    13→    PLAN_VERSION,
    14→    PlanModel,
    15→    empty_plan,
    16→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    17→    validate_plan,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import STATE_DIR, json_default
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→PLAN_FILE = STATE_DIR / "plan.json"
    25→
    26→
    27→def load_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> PlanModel:
    28→    """Load plan from disk, or return empty plan on missing/corruption."""
    29→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    30→    if not plan_path.exists():
    31→        return empty_plan()
    32→
    33→    try:
    34→        data = json.loads(plan_path.read_text())
    35→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
    36→        # Try backup before giving up
    37→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    38→        if backup.exists():
    39→            try:
    40→                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
    41→                logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s), loaded from backup.", ex)
    42→                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)
    43→                # Fall through to validation below
    44→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as backup_ex:
    45→                logger.warning("Plan file and backup both corrupted: %s / %s", ex, backup_ex)
    46→                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    47→                return empty_plan()
    48→        else:
    49→            logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s). Starting fresh.", ex)
    50→            print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    51→            return empty_plan()
    52→
    53→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    54→        logger.warning("Plan file root is not a JSON object. Starting fresh.")
    55→        print("  Warning: Plan file root must be a JSON object. Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    56→        return empty_plan()
    57→
    58→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    59→    if version > PLAN_VERSION:
    60→        logger.warning("Plan file version %d > supported %d.", version, PLAN_VERSION)
    61→        print(
    62→            f"  Warning: Plan file version {version} is newer than supported "
    63→            f"({PLAN_VERSION}). Some features may not work correctly.",
    64→            file=sys.stderr,
    65→        )
    66→
    67→    ensure_plan_defaults(data)
    68→    try:
    69→        validate_plan(data)
    70→    except ValueError as ex:
    71→        logger.warning("Plan invariants invalid (%s). Starting fresh.", ex)
    72→        print(f"  Warning: Plan invariants invalid ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    73→        return empty_plan()
    74→
    75→    return data  # type: ignore[return-value]
    76→
    77→
    78→def save_plan(plan: PlanModel | dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    79→    """Validate and save plan to disk atomically."""
    80→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    81→    plan["updated"] = utc_now()
    82→    validate_plan(plan)
    83→
    84→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    85→    plan_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    86→
    87→    content = json.dumps(plan, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
    88→
    89→    if plan_path.exists():
    90→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    91→        try:
    92→            shutil.copy2(str(plan_path), str(backup))
    93→        except OSError as backup_ex:
    94→            logger.debug("Failed to create plan backup: %s", backup_ex)
    95→
    96→    try:
    97→        safe_write_text(plan_path, content)
    98→    except OSError as ex:
    99→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save plan: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   100→        raise
```

```
1→"""Unified subjective-visibility policy.
     2→
     3→A single frozen dataclass computed once per operation replaces the scattered
     4→``has_objective_items`` / ``objective_count`` computations in
     5→``stale_dimensions``, ``auto_cluster``, and ``_work_queue/core``.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→# Detectors whose findings are NOT objective mechanical work.
    15→# Canonical definition — re-exported by stale_dimensions for back-compat.
    16→NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    17→    "review", "concerns", "subjective_review", "subjective_assessment",
    18→})
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    22→class SubjectiveVisibility:
    23→    """Immutable snapshot of the subjective-vs-objective balance."""
    24→
    25→    has_objective_backlog: bool  # any open non-subjective findings?
    26→    objective_count: int  # how many
    27→    unscored_ids: frozenset[str]  # subjective::* IDs needing initial review
    28→    stale_ids: frozenset[str]  # subjective::* IDs needing re-review
    29→    under_target_ids: frozenset[str]  # below target, not stale/unscored
    30→
    31→    def should_surface(self, item: dict) -> bool:
    32→        """Should this subjective queue item appear in the work queue?
    33→
    34→        Unscored (initial_review) -> always.  All others -> only when drained.
    35→        """
    36→        if item.get("initial_review"):
    37→            return True
    38→        return not self.has_objective_backlog
    39→
    40→    def should_inject_to_plan(self, fid: str) -> bool:
    41→        """Should this subjective ID be injected into plan queue_order?"""
    42→        if fid in self.unscored_ids:
    43→            return True  # unconditional
    44→        if fid in self.stale_ids:
    45→            return not self.has_objective_backlog
    46→        if fid in self.under_target_ids:
    47→            return not self.has_objective_backlog
    48→        return False
    49→
    50→    def should_evict_from_plan(self, fid: str) -> bool:
    51→        """Should this subjective ID be removed from plan queue_order?"""
    52→        if fid in self.unscored_ids:
    53→            return False  # never evict unscored
    54→        if fid in self.stale_ids or fid in self.under_target_ids:
    55→            return self.has_objective_backlog
    56→        return False
    57→
    58→    @property
    59→    def backlog_blocks_rerun(self) -> bool:
    60→        """Preflight: should reruns be blocked?"""
    61→        return self.has_objective_backlog
    62→
    63→
    64→def _is_evidence_only(finding: dict) -> bool:
    65→    """Return True if the finding is below its detector's standalone threshold."""
    66→    from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    67→    from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    68→
    69→    detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    70→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
    71→    if meta and meta.standalone_threshold:
    72→        threshold_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(meta.standalone_threshold, 9)
    73→        finding_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(finding.get("confidence", "low"), 9)
    74→        if finding_rank > threshold_rank:
    75→            return True
    76→    return False
    77→
    78→
    79→def compute_subjective_visibility(
    80→    state: StateModel,
    81→    *,
    82→    target_strict: float = 95.0,
    83→) -> SubjectiveVisibility:
    84→    """Build the policy snapshot from current state.
    85→
    86→    Imports building-block helpers from ``stale_dimensions`` so the
    87→    source-of-truth logic stays in one place.
    88→    """
    89→    from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    90→        _current_stale_ids,
    91→        current_under_target_ids,
    92→        current_unscored_ids,
    93→    )
    94→
    95→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    96→
    97→    # Count open, non-suppressed, objective findings.
    98→    # Evidence-only findings (below standalone confidence threshold) are
    99→    # excluded — they still affect scores but are not actionable queue items.
   100→    objective_count = sum(
   101→        1
   102→        for f in findings.values()
   103→        if f.get("status") == "open"
   104→        and f.get("detector") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   105→        and not f.get("suppressed")
   106→        and not _is_evidence_only(f)
   107→    )
   108→
   109→    unscored = current_unscored_ids(state)
   110→    stale = _current_stale_ids(state)
   111→    under_target = current_under_target_ids(state, target_strict=target_strict)
   112→
   113→    return SubjectiveVisibility(
   114→        has_objective_backlog=objective_count > 0,
   115→        objective_count=objective_count,
   116→        unscored_ids=frozenset(unscored),
   117→        stale_ids=frozenset(stale),
   118→        under_target_ids=frozenset(under_target),
   119→    )
   120→
   121→
   122→__all__ = [
   123→    "NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS",
   124→    "SubjectiveVisibility",
   125→    "compute_subjective_visibility",
   126→]
   127→
```

## Comprehensive Exploration of `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/`

I've thoroughly analyzed all six Python files in the `_work_queue` package (1,310 lines total). Here's the complete breakdown:

### **FILE 1: `__init__.py` (1 line)**

### **FILE 2: `core.py` (192 lines)**

**Responsibility:** Unified work-queue orchestration. This is the **main entry point** — it orchestrates all filtering, enrichment, ordering, and synthesis logic to build a final ranked queue.

**Public Functions:** 2
- `build_work_queue(state, options)` — The main orchestrator
- (Exports via `__all__`: `build_work_queue`, `QueueBuildOptions`, `WorkQueueResult`, `group_queue_items`)

**Data Classes/Types:**
- `QueueBuildOptions` (frozen dataclass) — configuration with 13 fields
- `WorkQueueResult` (TypedDict) — output schema with `items`, `total`, `grouped`, `new_ids`

**Imports from same package:**
- `helpers`: `ALL_STATUSES`, `ATTEST_EXAMPLE`, `build_create_plan_item`, `build_score_checkpoint_item`, `build_subjective_items`, `build_triage_stage_items`, `scope_matches` (7 imports)
- `ranking`: `build_finding_items`, `enrich_with_impact`, `group_queue_items`, `item_explain`, `item_sort_key` (5 imports)
- `plan_order`: `apply_plan_order`, `collapse_clusters`, `new_item_ids` (3 imports, prefixed with `_`)

**Filtering Logic:**
- Status filter validation (line 72–74)
- Subjective threshold clamping (line 76–79)
- Subjective visibility gating (lines 111–120) — respects plan override
- Impact floor: drops mechanical findings with impact < 0.05 (lines 135–144)

**Ordering Logic:**
- Calls `item_sort_key()` from `ranking` (line 146)
- Plan-order application via `_apply_plan_order()` (lines 152–158)
- Cluster collapse via `_collapse_clusters()` (line 168)

**Enrichment Logic:**
- Calls `enrich_with_impact()` to stamp `estimated_impact` (line 133)
- Adds `explain` field if requested (lines 174–176)
- Injects subjective items with visibility/scoring context (lines 99–120)
- Synthetic item injection: triage stages, workflow checkpoints, create-plan items (lines 123–131)

**Observations:**
- This is the **main gate**: all items flow through here
- Heavy orchestration of sub-modules; core logic is delegated
- Count/slicing happens last (lines 171–172)

---

### **FILE 3: `helpers.py` (462 lines)**

**Responsibility:** Building synthetic work items and utility predicates. Provides:
- Detection helpers (`is_subjective_finding()`, `is_review_finding()`, etc.)
- Item construction (`build_subjective_items()`, `build_triage_stage_items()`, `build_score_checkpoint_item()`, `build_create_plan_item()`)
- Scoring utilities (`subjective_strict_scores()`)
- Scope matching (`scope_matches()`)

**Public Functions:** 16 (via `__all__`)
1. `detail_dict(item)` — safely extract finding detail dict
2. `status_matches(item_status, status_filter)` — boolean status predicate
3. `is_subjective_finding(item)` — detector-based check
4. `is_review_finding(item)` — detector-based check
5. `is_subjective_queue_item(item)` — includes collapsed clusters
6. `review_finding_weight(item)` — returns float for review findings
7. `scope_matches(item, scope)` — pattern/prefix matching (fnmatch, hash suffix, path startswith)
8. `slugify(text)` — normalize text to snake_case
9. `supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)` — extract fixer set from state by language
10. `primary_command_for_finding(item, supported_fixers)` — map detector/type to a CLI command
11. `build_triage_stage_items(plan, state)` — synthesize triage::* workflow items
12. `build_score_checkpoint_item(plan, state)` — synthesize workflow::score-checkpoint item
13. `build_create_plan_item(plan)` — synthesize workflow::create-plan item
14. `build_subjective_items(state, findings, threshold)` — synthesize subjective dimension items
15. `subjective_strict_scores(state)` — extract dimension scores lookup

**Imports from same package:** None — self-contained

**Private Helpers (not exported):**

**Enrichment Logic:**

- `build_triage_stage_items()` — 70 lines, complex: injects triage::observe/reflect/organize/commit stages, computes blocked_by dependencies

### **FILE 4: `issues.py` (106 lines)**

**Responsibility:** Review findings state management — listing, sorting by impact weight, updating investigation notes, expiring stale holistic findings.

**Public Functions:** 4

2. `list_open_review_findings(state)` — return open review findings sorted by impact (highest first)
3. `update_investigation(state, finding_id, text)` — store investigation note on finding
4. `expire_stale_holistic(state, max_age_days)` — auto-resolve old holistic review findings

**Imports from same package:**
- `helpers`: `detail_dict` (1 import)

**Filtering Logic:**
- `list_open_review_findings()` — filters by status=="open" and detector=="review"
- `expire_stale_holistic()` — filters by detector=="review", status=="open", detail.holistic==true, age > 30 days

**Ordering Logic:**
- `list_open_review_findings()` — sorts by `(-weight, finding_id)` (descending weight, then ID)

**Mutations:**
- `update_investigation()` — modifies finding detail dict in-place
- `expire_stale_holistic()` — modifies finding status, resolved_at, note in-place; returns list of expired IDs

**Observations:**
- Narrow scope: **review findings only**
- Performs **state mutations** (not pure functions like ranking)
- Weight calculation delegates to `desloppify.core.issues_render.finding_weight()`
- Used by external commands for investigation UX; not part of core queue build

---

### **FILE 5: `plan_order.py` (241 lines)**

**Responsibility:** Living plan application and cluster collapse. Takes a sorted queue and reorders it per plan, applies plan metadata (descriptions, notes, cluster info), filters by cluster drilling, and collapses cluster members into meta-items.

**Public Functions:** 3
1. `new_item_ids(state)` — return finding IDs added in most recent scan
2. `apply_plan_order(items, plan, include_skipped, cluster, new_ids)` — reorder items per plan, apply overrides, partition into ordered/remaining/new/skipped
3. `collapse_clusters(items, plan)` — replace cluster member items with single meta-items

**Imports from same package:** None

**Private Helpers:**
- `action_type_for_detector(detector)` — look up detector metadata
- `_build_cluster_meta(cluster_name, members, cluster_data)` — synthesize cluster meta-item from members

**Filtering Logic:**
- `apply_plan_order()` — filters by skipped_ids, partitions by new_ids (line 68–78)
- `collapse_clusters()` — filters to auto-clusters only (line 197–198), requires 2+ members to collapse (line 215)

**Ordering Logic:**
- `apply_plan_order()` — respects `queue_order` list from plan (lines 60–63)
- Re-sorts workflow stages in-place to restore dependency order (lines 85–98)
- Queue position stamping (lines 103–105)

**Enrichment Logic:**
- `apply_plan_order()` — stamps plan_description, plan_note, plan_cluster, plan_skipped, plan_skip_kind, plan_skip_reason onto items (lines 43–112)
- Cluster drilling: if `cluster` param or `active_cluster`, filters to cluster member IDs only (lines 114–119)

**Observations:**
- **Plan authority**: plan.queue_order is the canonical order
- Cluster collapse is **non-destructive**: walks in order, replaces first member, skips rest
- Stage dependency re-sort (lines 85–98) suggests triage stages can get scrambled by plan order
- Returns partition: `ordered + remaining_existing + remaining_new [+ skipped_items]`

---

### **FILE 6: `ranking.py` (308 lines)**

**Responsibility:** Impact computation, sort-key generation, item explanation, grouping, and finding-to-item conversion. Provides the **ranking** and **enrichment** layer.

**Public Functions:** 6
1. `enrich_with_impact(items, dimension_scores)` — stamp `estimated_impact` field on all items based on dimension headroom
2. `build_finding_items(state, scan_path, status_filter, scope, chronic)` — convert findings dict to queue items with enrichment
3. `item_sort_key(item)` — return sort tuple for ranking
4. `item_explain(item)` — return dict explaining why an item ranks where it does
5. `subjective_score_value(item)` — extract subjective score (for sort)
6. `group_queue_items(items, group)` — group items by file/detector/cluster/default

**Imports from same package:**
- `helpers`: `ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY`, `detail_dict`, `is_review_finding`, `is_subjective_finding`, `primary_command_for_finding`, `review_finding_weight`, `scope_matches`, `slugify`, `status_matches`, `subjective_strict_scores`, `supported_fixers_for_item` (11 imports)

**Private Helpers:**
- `_compute_item_impact(item, dim_impact, get_dimension_for_detector)` — compute impact value for single item

**Filtering Logic:**
- `build_finding_items()` — filters by:
  - suppressed flag (line 105)
  - status filter (lines 107–108)
  - chronic flag (lines 109–112)
  - `standalone_threshold` from detector registry (lines 114–121) — **evidence-only filtering**
  - `scope_matches()` (lines 146–147)

**Ordering Logic:**
- `item_sort_key()` — complex multi-tier sort:
  - **Initial-review subjective**: tier -3 (highest priority)
  - **Workflow stages**: tier -2, then blocked penalty, stage order
  - **Workflow actions**: tier -1
  - **Clusters**: tier 0, by action_type, by member count desc
  - **Findings (mechanical/subjective/review)**: tier 1, by impact desc, confidence asc, (review weight or subjective score), count desc

**Enrichment Logic:**
- `build_finding_items()` — adds:
  - id, kind, is_review, is_subjective
  - review_weight (if review)
  - subjective_score (if subjective)
  - primary_command (CLI hint)
  - estimated_impact (stamped by caller via `enrich_with_impact()`)
- `enrich_with_impact()` — computes per-item impact based on dimension scores and headroom
- `item_explain()` — returns rich explanation dict with policy, ranking_factors

**Observations:**
- **Heavy lifting**: 308 lines, implements core ranking logic
- Sort key is **multi-tier**: synthetic items (stages, workflows) sort by tier first, then within tier
- `item_sort_key()` is the canonical ranking policy
- Evidence-only filtering (standalone_threshold) is in here — mechanical findings below confidence threshold are hidden
- `enrich_with_impact()` uses dimension score headroom (100 - score) as key metric

---

### **2. QUEUEBUILDOPTIONS FIELDS: ACTUAL VS. DECLARED**

**Declared fields** (12 total):
```python
count: int | None = 1
scan_path: str | None = None
scope: str | None = None
status: str = "open"
include_subjective: bool = True
subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
chronic: bool = False
explain: bool = False
plan: dict | None = None
include_skipped: bool = False
cluster: str | None = None
collapse_clusters: bool = True
policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None
```

**Actually used across all call sites:**

| Field | Usage Count | Notes |
|-------|------------|-------|
| `status` | 11/18 | Always set explicitly (never uses default) |
| `count` | 10/18 | Very common; mixed defaults (1, None, varies) |
| `include_subjective` | 10/18 | Common control flag |
| `plan` | 8/18 | Only in commands/planning code (not in helpers) |
| `scan_path` | 7/18 | Usually pulled from state automatically |

## **KEY INSIGHTS FOR COMPLEXITY REDUCTION**

### **Actual vs. Theoretical Complexity**

The 12-field `QueueBuildOptions` could reasonably be **split into 2-3 focused configs**:

1. **Core Selection** (used by ALL callers):
   - `status` (open, fixed, wontfix, all)
   - `count` (pagination)
   - `scan_path` (usually implicit from state)

2. **Content Control** (used by most callers):
   - `include_subjective` (boolean gate on synthetic items)
   - `subjective_threshold` (score cutoff)

3. **Plan Integration** (used by ~40% of callers):
   - `plan` (living plan data)
   - `include_skipped` (visibility flag)
   - `cluster` (focus filter)
   - `collapse_clusters` (meta-item control)

4. **Niche Options** (used by 1 caller each):
   - `explain` (only next.py — for CLI explanation text)
   - `chronic` (only show/scope.py — legacy filtering mode)
   - `policy` (only preflight.py — custom subjective visibility)
   - `scope` (only next.py + show/scope.py — file pattern matching)

### **Most Common Minimal Calls**

The helpers typically call with **4-5 fields**:
- `status` 
- `count`
- `include_subjective`
- Sometimes: `plan`, `collapse_clusters`

### **Command-Level Calls** 

Only `next.py` uses the full complexity (10 fields), because it's the user-facing "show next item" command that needs all the options.

## Comprehensive Analysis: Plan-Queue Coupling in Desloppify

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's what I found regarding the plan-queue interaction and where unnecessary complexity exists:

### 1. **Plan Module Architecture** (`desloppify/engine/_plan/`)

The plan system has **11 files** with distinct responsibilities:

| File | Responsibility |
|------|-----------------|
| `schema.py` | TypedDict definitions (PlanModel, SkipEntry, Cluster, etc.), version management, validation |
| `persistence.py` | Load/save with atomic writes + backups |
| `operations.py` | Pure mutations: move_items, skip_items, create_cluster, etc. |
| `reconcile.py` | Sync plan with state after scan/review changes |
| `auto_cluster.py` | Auto-group findings by detector/signature |
| `commit_tracking.py` | Track which findings are uncommitted |
| `stale_dimensions.py` | Determine which subjective dimensions need attention |
| `subjective_policy.py` | Unify visibility rules for subjective vs objective findings |
| `epic_triage.py` | Multi-stage triage workflow management |
| `schema_migrations.py` | Forward compatibility for plan versions |

The plan stores:
- `queue_order`: ordered list of finding IDs
- `skipped`: dict mapping IDs to SkipEntry (reason, kind, attestation)
- `overrides`: dict with descriptions and notes
- `clusters`: named groups with metadata
- `active_cluster`: focus mode
- `promoted_ids`: IDs explicitly moved to top
- `execution_log`: audit trail
- Various triage/commit metadata

### 2. **Work Queue Module Architecture** (`desloppify/engine/_work_queue/`)

**6 files** with coupling to plan:

| File | Responsibility | Plan Coupling |
|------|-----------------|--------------|
| `core.py` | `build_work_queue()` orchestrator | **HIGH**: conditionally includes plan in options |
| `ranking.py` | Sort key computation (impact, confidence, etc.) | **NONE**: pure ranking |
| `helpers.py` | Item construction, subjective items, triage stages | **MEDIUM**: builds synthetic items from plan state |
| `plan_order.py` | `apply_plan_order()` and `collapse_clusters()` | **CRITICAL**: this is the reordering pass |
| `issues.py` | Review findings manipulation | **NONE** |
| `__init__.py` | Re-exports | **NONE** |

### 3. **Key Coupling Points Identified**

#### **Point A: `apply_plan_order()` (plan_order.py, lines 23-120)**

This function is the **primary coupling mechanism**:

```python
def apply_plan_order(
    items: list[dict],
    plan: dict,
    *,
    include_skipped: bool = False,
    cluster: str | None = None,
    new_ids: set[str] | None = None,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Reorder items according to the living plan."""
```

**What it does:**
1. Builds a by-id map of items
2. Applies override metadata (descriptions, notes, cluster membership)
3. Filters items based on `queue_order` and `skipped` 
4. Restores stage ordering for workflow items (lines 82-98)
5. Optionally includes skipped items
6. Adds `queue_position`, `plan_skipped`, `plan_skip_kind`, `plan_cluster` fields
7. Filters to active cluster members if focused

**Problem: This is a SEPARATE REORDERING PASS after `item_sort_key()` already ranked everything.**

Items are sorted by `item_sort_key()` (lines 146 in core.py), then immediately re-sorted by this pass. The reordering is **not composable with the ranking logic**—it's a hard reorder + filter pipeline.

#### **Point B: `_resolve_plan_context()` (queue_render.py, lines 21-31)**

```python
def _resolve_plan_context(plan: dict, cluster_filter: str | None) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None]:
    plan_data: dict | None = None
    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
        plan_data = plan
    
    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
    if plan_data and not cluster_filter:
        active_cluster = plan_data.get("active_cluster")
        if active_cluster:
            effective_cluster = active_cluster
    return plan_data, effective_cluster
```

**Decision logic:** "Is the plan 'meaningful'?" It returns `plan_data=None` if the queue is empty AND there are no overrides AND no clusters. This is used by `cmd_plan_queue` to decide whether to apply plan logic.

**Problem: This is UNDER-SPECIFIED.** It should be "does the user have a living plan?" but instead it infers from content. What if someone has only a few skipped items? The condition works but is implicit.

#### **Point C: Subjective gating in `build_work_queue()` (core.py, lines 94-120)**

```python
if (
    resolved_options.include_subjective
    and status in {"open", "all"}
    and not resolved_options.chronic
):
    subjective_items = build_subjective_items(...)
    plan_queue_set: set[str] = (
        set(resolved_options.plan.get("queue_order", []))
        if resolved_options.plan
        else set()
    )
    for item in subjective_items:
        ...
        # Plan overrides policy: "the plan is authoritative"
        item_id = item.get("id", "")
        if not policy.should_surface(item) and item_id not in plan_queue_set:
            continue
```

**Decision:** If plan includes a subjective item in `queue_order`, surface it regardless of the visibility policy. This is an **override exception**.

**Problem: Mixed responsibility.** The queue builder has to know about plan exceptions to gating logic. This should be pushed up to the caller or baked into the policy object.

#### **Point D: Cluster collapse (core.py, lines 160-168)**

```python
should_collapse = (
    resolved_options.collapse_clusters
    and resolved_options.plan
    and not resolved_options.cluster
    and not resolved_options.plan.get("active_cluster")
)
if should_collapse:
    all_items = _collapse_clusters(all_items, resolved_options.plan)
```

**Decision:** Only collapse when NOT drilling into a cluster and NOT in focused mode.

**What `collapse_clusters()` does** (lines 184-234):
- Finds all auto-clusters in plan
- Maps finding IDs to cluster names
- For each cluster, builds synthetic meta-item with aggregated member data
- Replaces first member with meta-item, skips subsequent members

**Problem: This is O(n²) in item count + requires separate knowledge of cluster boundaries.** And it **mutates item order** a second time.

#### **Point E: Triage/workflow item injection (core.py, lines 122-131)**

```python
if resolved_options.plan and status in {"open", "all"}:
    synth_items = build_triage_stage_items(resolved_options.plan, state)
    all_items.extend(synth_items)
    checkpoint_item = build_score_checkpoint_item(resolved_options.plan, state)
    if checkpoint_item is not None:
        all_items.append(checkpoint_item)
    plan_item = build_create_plan_item(resolved_options.plan)
    if plan_item is not None:
        all_items.append(plan_item)
```

**What happens:**
- `build_triage_stage_items()` (helpers.py, lines 184-254): Creates synthetic items for each `triage::*` stage in `queue_order`. Also re-sorts these stage items in-place (lines 85-98 in plan_order.py) to respect dependency order.
- `build_score_checkpoint_item()`: Creates meta-item if `workflow::score-checkpoint` is in queue.
- `build_create_plan_item()`: Creates meta-item if `workflow::create-plan` is in queue.

**Problem: TIGHT COUPLING.** The queue builder must know about plan-specific synthetic IDs and when to inject them. This logic is scattered across helpers and plan_order.

### 4. **Is the Complexity Necessary?**

**YES and NO:**

**Necessary complexity:**
- Plan-based reordering is core: users explicitly set priority via `plan move`
- Skipped items must be filtered out (or marked) — state-layer-only would lose metadata
- Cluster collapse is useful UX (avoid item avalanche)
- Triage workflow items must inject into the queue
- Subjective gating needs plan override (user forcing inclusion)

**Over-engineered complexity:**
1. **`apply_plan_order()` as a separate pass** — Could be:
   - A sort key component: `(in_queue_order_position, impact, confidence, ...)`
   - No need for a full reorder-and-filter pipeline
   - Stage re-sorting (lines 82-98) shouldn't be needed if stages have sort key that respects dependency

2. **`_resolve_plan_context()` implicit logic** — Should be:
   - `has_living_plan()` predicate in plan.py (already exists!)
   - Not inferred from content

3. **Triage/workflow injection in core.py** — Should be:
   - Handled at a higher level: "add plan items before building work queue"
   - Or: "plan provides pre-constructed items that get merged into queue"

4. **Cluster collapse as post-process** — Should be:
   - A rendering-layer concern, not a queue-building concern
   - Pass `item.plan_cluster` metadata but don't collapse here
   - Let the UI decide whether to show collapsed or expanded

5. **Subjective gating exception in core.py** — Should be:
   - Built into `SubjectiveVisibility` policy object
   - Policy receives plan context at construction time
   - `policy.should_surface(item, plan_context)` is clean

### 5. **Coupling Summary Table**

| Coupling Point | Location | Strength | Direction | Root Cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Queue ordering | `plan_order.py:23-120` | CRITICAL | Plan→Queue | Reorder pass instead of sort key |
| Plan data validation | `queue_render.py:21-31` | MEDIUM | Command→Plan | Implicit content-based check |
| Subjective override | `core.py:104-119` | HIGH | Plan→Queue gating | Exception hardcoded in builder |
| Cluster collapse | `core.py:160-168` | HIGH | Plan→Queue rendering | Post-process mutation |
| Triage injection | `core.py:122-131` | HIGH | Plan→Queue item construction | Synthetic IDs in queue logic |
| Stage re-sorting | `plan_order.py:82-98` | MEDIUM | Plan→Queue ordering | Dependency tracking in reorder |

### 6. **Concrete Refactoring Opportunities**

**Opportunity 1: Make plan position a sort key component**
```python
def apply_plan_order_sort_key(item_id: str, plan: dict) -> tuple:
    # Returns (position_in_queue_order, is_promoted, is_skipped)
    # Compose with other keys: (plan_position, impact, confidence, ...)
```
Eliminates the separate `apply_plan_order()` pass entirely. The plan becomes a sort tie-breaker, not a reorder.

**Opportunity 2: Extract subjective policy computation earlier**
```python
# In build_work_queue():
policy = compute_subjective_visibility(state, plan=plan)  # pass plan
```
Teach the policy about plan overrides at construction time. No exceptions in the builder.

**Opportunity 3: Separate rendering from queue logic**
```python
# build_work_queue() returns:
# - items: list of base items (no cluster collapse)
# - metadata: cluster info, stage dependencies, etc.

# Rendering layer decides:
# - collapse_clusters(items, metadata) → for compact display
# - item_explain(item) → already does this!
```

**Opportunity 4: Move synthetic item construction outside queue builder**
```python
def inject_plan_items(items: list[dict], plan: dict, state: dict) -> list[dict]:
    """Add triage/workflow items before queue building."""
    triage_items = build_triage_stage_items(plan, state)
    return triage_items + items

# Then: synth_items = inject_plan_items([], plan, state)
# And: all_items = synth_items + build_work_queue(...)
```

### 7. **Commands That Touch Queue vs Plan**

Plan subcommands in `app/commands/plan/cmd.py`:
- `show` — metadata summary
- `queue` — render compact table (uses `_resolve_plan_context`)
- `reset` — clear everything
- `move` — reorder items
- `describe`, `note` — add metadata
- `focus` — set active cluster
- `skip`, `unskip` — toggle skipped status
- `done`, `reopen` — mark findings resolved
- `cluster` — group findings
- `triage` — multi-stage workflow
- `commit-log` — track resolved findings

**All of these call operations from `engine/_plan/operations.py`**, which are pure mutations. Good isolation.

Then `cmd_plan_queue` calls `build_work_queue()` with plan context. That's where coupling happens.

### 8. **Bottom Line**

**The plan-queue entanglement is REAL but mostly in:**
1. **`apply_plan_order()` as a separate reordering pass** — Could be a sort key
2. **Subjective gating exception** — Should be in policy object
3. **Synthetic item injection** — Should be pre-queue, not mid-builder
4. **Cluster collapse** — Should be rendering-only, not queue-building

**The operations layer is CLEAN:**
- `move_items()`, `skip_items()`, etc. are pure, testable mutations
- No circular dependencies
- Easy to reason about state changes

**The decoupling strategy would be:**
1. Merge plan ordering into `item_sort_key()` as a component
2. Push subjective policy exceptions to `SubjectiveVisibility` constructor
3. Pre-inject synthetic items before queue building
4. Move cluster collapse to rendering layer (query.json, show, plan queue, etc.)

This would reduce the "queue builder as a kitchen sink" pattern and make plan a cleaner **sort-key component** rather than a **separate ordering system**.

## COMPLETE ANALYSIS: `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/` Package

### SUMMARY TABLE

| File | LOC | Responsibility | Public Functions | Exports | Key Imports from Package |
|------|-----|-----------------|------------------|---------|--------------------------|
| `__init__.py` | 1 | Package stub | 0 | (none) | — |
| `core.py` | 192 | Orchestration | 2 | build_work_queue, QueueBuildOptions, WorkQueueResult, group_queue_items | helpers (7), ranking (5), plan_order (3) |
| `helpers.py` | 462 | Synthetic items, predicates | 15 | (16 in __all__) | — |
| `issues.py` | 106 | Review findings state mgmt | 4 | (all exported) | helpers (1) |
| `plan_order.py` | 241 | Living plan application | 3 | (all exported) | — |
| `ranking.py` | 308 | Impact, ranking, grouping | 6 | (all exported) | helpers (11) |
| **TOTAL** | **1,310** | | **30+ functions** | | |

---

### DETAILED LAYER ANALYSIS

#### **Layer 1: Core Orchestrator (`core.py`)**

Entry point: `build_work_queue(state, options) -> WorkQueueResult`

**Execution flow:**
1. **Validation** (lines 72–79): Validate status filter and subjective threshold
2. **Finding items** (lines 81–87): Call `build_finding_items()` from ranking (filtering + enrichment)
3. **Subjective items** (lines 99–120): Call `build_subjective_items()` from helpers (synthetic items + visibility gating)
4. **Synthetic items** (lines 123–131): Inject triage stages, score checkpoint, create-plan items from helpers
5. **Impact enrichment** (line 133): Call `enrich_with_impact()` from ranking (adds estimated_impact)
6. **Impact floor** (lines 135–144): **Filter out mechanical findings with impact < 0.05**
7. **Sort** (line 146): Call `item_sort_key()` from ranking (multi-tier ranking)
8. **Plan order** (lines 152–158): Call `_apply_plan_order()` from plan_order (reorder + metadata)
9. **Cluster collapse** (line 168): Call `_collapse_clusters()` from plan_order (meta-items)
10. **Slicing** (lines 171–172): Apply count limit
11. **Explain** (lines 174–176): Add explain field if requested
12. **Output** (lines 178–183): Return WorkQueueResult dict

**Configuration via QueueBuildOptions:**
- `count`, `scan_path`, `scope`, `status`, `include_subjective`, `subjective_threshold`, `chronic`, `explain`, `plan`, `include_skipped`, `cluster`, `collapse_clusters`, `policy`

**Key observation:** This is purely **orchestration** — all logic is delegated.

---

#### **Layer 2: Detection & Synthesis (`helpers.py`)**

**Two distinct sublayers:**

**2a. Predicates & utilities:**
- `detail_dict()` — safe access to finding.detail
- `status_matches()` — simple boolean
- `is_subjective_finding()` — detector in {subjective_assessment, holistic_review}
- `is_review_finding()` — detector == "review"
- `is_subjective_queue_item()` — kind == subjective_dimension OR cluster with all subjective members
- `review_finding_weight()` — returns float (confidence weight × holistic bonus)
- `scope_matches()` — **sophisticated**: fnmatch, hash suffix, path prefix, detector name
- `slugify()` — text → snake_case
- `supported_fixers_for_item()` — extract fixer set by language
- `primary_command_for_finding()` — map detector/type → CLI command string

**2b. Synthetic item builders:**
- `build_triage_stage_items()` — 70 lines
  - Reads triage::* IDs from plan.queue_order
  - Computes blocked_by dependencies (dependency stages in queue but not confirmed)
  - Creates one item per stage with stage_name, primary_command, blocked_by
- `build_score_checkpoint_item()` — 35 lines
  - Creates one workflow action if WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID in queue_order
  - Includes delta from plan_start_scores
- `build_create_plan_item()` — 23 lines
  - Creates one workflow action if WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID in queue_order
- `build_subjective_items()` — 95 lines
  - **Most complex**: reads all_subjective_entries, computes unassessed_dims, counts review findings per dimension
  - For each dimension below threshold:
    - Creates synthetic item with kind=subjective_dimension
    - Sets primary_command based on open review findings (show review vs. review --prepare)
    - Flags as initial_review or stale_review
    - Returns list of synthetic dimension items

**2c. Dimension scoring:**
- `subjective_strict_scores()` — 24 lines
  - Reads dimension_scores from state
  - Maps dimension names to multiple aliases (display name, snake_case, slugified, canonical key)
  - Returns dict[alias] → score for fast lookup

**Constants:**
- `ALL_STATUSES` — set of finding_status_tokens
- `ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY` — {auto_fix: 0, refactor: 1, manual_fix: 2, reorganize: 3}
- `ATTEST_EXAMPLE` — template string

**Key observation:** This file is a **junk drawer** combining predicates, utilities, and complex item builders. Candidate for refactoring into multiple modules.

---

#### **Layer 3: Ranking & Enrichment (`ranking.py`)**

**3a. Impact computation:**
- `enrich_with_impact(items, dimension_scores)` — 30 lines
  - Computes health breakdown from dimension_scores
  - For each dimension: per_point = overall_per_point, headroom = 100 - score
  - For each item: calls `_compute_item_impact()` to get per_point × headroom
  - Stamps `estimated_impact` on every item

- `_compute_item_impact()` — 25 lines
  - **Subjective dimension**: look up by detail.dimension_name
  - **Mechanical finding**: use detector → dimension mapping
  - Returns float (per_point × headroom) or 0.0

**3b. Finding-to-item conversion:**
- `build_finding_items()` — 50 lines
  - Scopes to scan_path
  - Filters by: suppressed, status_filter, chronic (reopen_count >= 2), **standalone_threshold**, scope_matches
  - **Evidence-only filtering** (lines 114–121): skip mechanical findings below confidence threshold
  - Enriches each finding with: id, kind, is_review, is_subjective, review_weight, subjective_score, primary_command
  - Returns list of enriched finding items

**3c. Ranking logic:**
- `item_sort_key(item)` — 50 lines, **the canonical ranking policy**
  - **Tier -3**: initial-review subjective dimensions (highest priority, onboarding)
  - **Tier -2**: workflow stages (triage::*, sorted by blocked_penalty then stage_index)
  - **Tier -1**: workflow actions (e.g., create-plan)
  - **Tier 0**: clusters (by action_type, then member_count desc)
  - **Tier 1**: mechanical/subjective/review findings (by estimated_impact desc, then confidence asc, review_weight/subjective_score, count desc)

- `subjective_score_value(item)` — extract score for sorting (default 100.0)

**3d. Explanation:**
- `item_explain(item)` — 75 lines
  - Returns dict with kind, policy, ranking_factors for each item type
  - Documents why an item ranks where it does

**3e. Grouping:**
- `group_queue_items(items, group)` — 15 lines
  - Groups by file, detector, cluster, or default ("items")
  - Returns dict[key] → list[items]

**Key observation:** This is the **ranking engine** — contains all sorting logic. `item_sort_key()` is the policy document.

---

#### **Layer 4: Review Findings State (`issues.py`)**

**Narrow scope: review findings only**

- `impact_label(weight)` — 10 lines
  - Converts numeric weight to "+" / "++" / "+++" label

- `list_open_review_findings(state)` — 15 lines
  - Filters findings by status=="open" and detector=="review"
  - Sorts by (-weight, id)
  - Returns sorted list

- `update_investigation(state, finding_id, text)` — 12 lines
  - Stores investigation note on finding.detail
  - Returns False if not found/not open
  - **Mutates state in-place**

- `expire_stale_holistic(state, max_age_days=30)` — 35 lines
  - Filters holistic review findings by age
  - Auto-resolves findings older than max_age_days
  - **Mutates state in-place**
  - Returns list of expired IDs

**Key observation:** This is **state mutation** logic, not part of the core queue build. Used by investigation/review commands.

---

#### **Layer 5: Plan Application (`plan_order.py`)**

**3 public functions:**

- `new_item_ids(state)` — 12 lines
  - Returns finding IDs added in most recent scan
  - Uses scan_history[-1].timestamp as threshold
  - Compares finding.first_seen >= threshold

- `apply_plan_order(items, plan, include_skipped, cluster, new_ids)` — 95 lines
  - **Plan authority**: respects plan.queue_order
  - Partitions items into: ordered (in queue_order), skipped, remaining_existing, remaining_new
  - Returns: ordered + remaining_existing + remaining_new [+ skipped]
  - **Enriches each item with**:
    - plan_description, plan_note (from plan.overrides)
    - plan_cluster (name, description, total_items)
    - plan_skipped, plan_skip_kind, plan_skip_reason
    - queue_position (1-indexed)
  - Re-sorts workflow stages in-place to restore dependency order (lines 85–98)
  - Filters to cluster members if cluster drilling (lines 114–119)

- `collapse_clusters(items, plan)` — 50 lines
  - Identifies auto-clusters (cluster.auto == true)
  - For clusters with 2+ members: creates meta-item via `_build_cluster_meta()`
  - Walks items in order: replaces first member with meta-item, skips rest
  - Preserves ordering established by sort + plan_order

**Private helpers:**
- `action_type_for_detector(detector)` — 8 lines, looks up DETECTORS registry
- `_build_cluster_meta(cluster_name, members, cluster_data)` — 30 lines
  - Synthesizes cluster meta-item from members
  - Computes summary, action_type, primary_command, estimated_impact

**Key observation:** Plan order is **definitive** — queue_order list overrides default ranking. Cluster collapse is **non-destructive** (first member → meta-item, rest skipped).

### COMPLEXITY POINTS

1. **Filtering spread across 3 modules:**
   - `ranking.build_finding_items()`: suppressed, status, chronic, evidence-only (standalone_threshold), scope
   - `core.build_work_queue()`: subjective visibility policy, impact floor (0.05)
   - `plan_order.apply_plan_order()`: skipped items, cluster filtering

2. **Ordering in 2 modules:**
   - `ranking.item_sort_key()`: multi-tier sort (initial logic)
   - `plan_order.apply_plan_order()`: respects plan.queue_order (override), re-sorts triage stages

3. **Enrichment spread across 4 modules:**
   - `ranking.build_finding_items()`: id, kind, is_review, is_subjective, review_weight, subjective_score, primary_command
   - `ranking.enrich_with_impact()`: estimated_impact
   - `plan_order.apply_plan_order()`: plan_description, plan_note, plan_cluster, plan_skipped, queue_position
   - `helpers.build_subjective_items()`: synthetic dimension items with full enrichment

4. **Synthetic item construction in helpers:**
   - `build_triage_stage_items()`: complex dependency tracking
   - `build_subjective_items()`: 95 lines, complex scoring/aliasing
   - `build_score_checkpoint_item()`: small, specialized
   - `build_create_plan_item()`: trivial (23 lines)

5. **Evidence-only filtering hidden in `ranking.build_finding_items()`:**
   - Lines 114–121: checks detector.standalone_threshold vs. finding confidence rank
   - This is **discovery logic** but lives in ranking, not with other discovery filters

6. **Cluster collapse non-destructive walk:**
   - First member → meta-item, rest skipped
   - Ordering preserved (walk in order)
   - But what happens if cluster members are non-contiguous?

### DECISION LOGIC LOCATION

**Where is each core decision made?**

| Decision | File | Lines |
|----------|------|-------|
| What findings to include | ranking (filtering) + core (visibility) | ranking: 105–147, core: 111–120, 135–144 |
| What order | ranking (sort_key) + plan_order (plan_order list) | ranking: 153–201, plan_order: 60–63 |
| What to add to findings | helpers (builders) + ranking (enrichment) + plan_order (enrichment) | helpers: 184–441, ranking: 23–82, plan_order: 43–112 |
| Initial review priority | ranking (tier -3 sort) | ranking: 157–158 |
| Triage stage ordering | plan_order (re-sort in-place) | plan_order: 85–98 |
| Cluster collapse | plan_order (first member → meta) | plan_order: 220–234 |
| Impact calculation | ranking (per_point × headroom) | ranking: 40–82 |
| Evidence-only filtering | ranking (standalone_threshold check) | ranking: 114–121 |
| Subjective visibility gating | core (policy check) | core: 111–120 |
| Impact floor (0.05) | core (drop small impact items) | core: 135–144 |

---

This is the complete shape of the `_work_queue` package. The key insight is that **filtering, ordering, and enrichment are spread across multiple layers**, with **plan order and synthetic items adding additional complexity**. The core challenge: understanding the complete queue-building pipeline requires reading 5 interdependent modules.

> DEVELOPER

I need to design a plan for simplifying the work queue system in desloppify. Here's the comprehensive analysis from my exploration:

## Current State

The work queue package (`desloppify/engine/_work_queue/`) has **1310 lines, 30+ functions across 5 files** for what is conceptually: "filter findings by status, sort them, return top N."

### Architecture
- `core.py` (192 lines): `build_work_queue()` orchestrator with 12-field `QueueBuildOptions` dataclass
- `helpers.py` (462 lines): Junk drawer — predicates, utilities, synthetic item builders, dimension scoring
- `ranking.py` (308 lines): `build_finding_items()` (filter+enrich), `item_sort_key()`, `enrich_with_impact()`, `item_explain()`
- `plan_order.py` (241 lines): `apply_plan_order()` (separate reordering pass), `collapse_clusters()`
- `issues.py` (106 lines): Review findings state mutation (unrelated to queue building)

### The core problems

1. **Filtering is spread across 3 modules:**
   - `ranking.build_finding_items()`: suppressed, status, chronic, evidence-only threshold, scope
   - `core.build_work_queue()`: subjective visibility policy, impact floor (0.05)
   - `plan_order.apply_plan_order()`: skipped items, cluster filtering

2. **Ordering happens twice:**
   - `ranking.item_sort_key()` sorts by impact/confidence/tier
   - `plan_order.apply_plan_order()` REORDERS by queue_order, partitions into ordered/existing/new
   - These are not composable — the second pass undoes the first for planned items

3. **`_resolve_plan_context()` in queue_render.py** is an implicit gate that decides if plan is "meaningful" by checking truthiness of queue_order/overrides/clusters. This creates a hidden behavioral fork.

4. **Cluster collapse** is done inside `build_work_queue()` but is purely a display concern. Only 3 of 18 callers want it.

5. **Subjective gating exception**: `core.py` has a hardcoded plan-override for subjective visibility policy (if item is in plan's queue_order, surface it regardless of policy).

6. **Enrichment in 4 places**: ranking (finding→item), ranking (impact), plan_order (plan metadata + queue position), helpers (synthetic items).

### What apply_plan_order actually does (plan_order.py:23-120):
1. Builds a by-id map from already-filtered items
2. Applies override metadata (descriptions, notes, cluster membership) to items
3. Builds ordered list: items in queue_order first (preserving plan order), then remaining existing items, then new items
4. Re-sorts workflow stages in-place for dependency order
5. Optionally appends skipped items
6. Stamps queue_position on all items
7. Filters to active cluster members if cluster focus is set

### The key insight
`apply_plan_order()` is a reordering + filtering + enrichment pass. This can be decomposed:
- **Enrichment** (plan metadata) → enrich step
- **Ordering** (queue_order position) → sort key component: `(0, position)` for planned items, `(1, *natural_key)` for existing, `(2, *natural_key)` for new
- **Filtering** (skipped, cluster) → main filtering step
- **queue_position** → just enumerate after final sort

### Callers of build_work_queue (18 total):
- Primary commands: next.py (10 fields), plan/queue_render.py (7), show/scope.py (6), preflight.py (4), queue_guard.py (4)
- Helpers: queue_progress.py (5, twice), select.py (4), queue_policy.py (5), render.py (5, twice)
- Tests: 5+ files

Most callers only use 3-5 fields. Only next.py uses the full complexity.

### Files I've read completely:
- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` — build_work_queue orchestrator
- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` — predicates + synthetic builders
- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` — filtering, sort key, impact
- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py` — plan reordering + cluster collapse
- `desloppify/engine/work_queue.py` — public facade, re-exports 30+ names
- `desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py` — plan queue command

## Design Constraints

1. Must not break existing tests (lots of test coverage in tests/review/work_queue_cases.py)
2. Must not change external behavior — same items in same order
3. Public API via work_queue.py facade should remain stable
4. Should be achievable in phases, each independently shippable
5. issues.py is unrelated to queue building — leave it alone

## What I need from you

Design a concrete, phased simplification plan. For each phase:
- What changes
- Which files are modified
- What the before/after looks like conceptually
- How to verify it didn't break anything

Focus on changes that maximize readability improvement per line changed. The goal is: "someone investigating a bug like 'wontfixed findings showing up' should be able to answer the question by reading ONE function, not tracing through 5 files."

> AGENT

I'll explore the codebase thoroughly to understand the current architecture, dependencies, and test coverage before designing the simplification plan.

```
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.subjective_policy import (
     9→    SubjectiveVisibility,
    10→    compute_subjective_visibility,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
    13→    ALL_STATUSES,
    14→    ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    15→    build_create_plan_item,

    29→    apply_plan_order as _apply_plan_order,
    30→    collapse_clusters as _collapse_clusters,
    31→    new_item_ids as _new_item_ids,
    32→)
    33→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    34→
    35→
    36→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    37→class QueueBuildOptions:
    38→    """Configuration for queue construction."""
    39→
    40→    count: int | None = 1
    41→    scan_path: str | None = None
    42→    scope: str | None = None
    43→    status: str = "open"
    44→    include_subjective: bool = True
    45→    subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
    46→    chronic: bool = False
    47→    explain: bool = False
    48→    plan: dict | None = None
    49→    include_skipped: bool = False
    50→    cluster: str | None = None
    51→    collapse_clusters: bool = True
    52→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None
    53→
    54→
    55→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    56→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    57→
    58→    items: list[dict]
    59→    total: int
    60→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]]
    61→    new_ids: set[str]
    62→
    63→
    64→def build_work_queue(
    65→    state: StateModel,
    66→    *,
    67→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    68→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    69→    """Build ranked queue items + tier metadata."""
    70→    resolved_options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    71→
    72→    status = resolved_options.status
    73→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
    74→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
    75→    try:
    76→        subjective_threshold_value = float(resolved_options.subjective_threshold)
    77→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    78→        subjective_threshold_value = 100.0
    79→    subjective_threshold_value = max(0.0, min(100.0, subjective_threshold_value))
    80→
    81→    finding_items = build_finding_items(
    82→        state,
    83→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
    84→        status_filter=status,
    85→        scope=resolved_options.scope,
    86→        chronic=resolved_options.chronic,
    87→    )
    88→
    89→    all_items = list(finding_items)
    90→
    91→    # Resolve subjective visibility policy for gating
    92→    policy = resolved_options.policy or compute_subjective_visibility(state)
    93→
    94→    if (
    95→        resolved_options.include_subjective
    96→        and status in {"open", "all"}
    97→        and not resolved_options.chronic
    98→    ):
    99→        subjective_items = build_subjective_items(
   100→            state,
   101→            state.get("findings", {}),
   102→            threshold=subjective_threshold_value,
   103→        )
   104→        # When a plan explicitly includes a subjective item in queue_order,
   105→        # surface it regardless of policy — the plan is authoritative.
   106→        plan_queue_set: set[str] = (
   107→            set(resolved_options.plan.get("queue_order", []))
   108→            if resolved_options.plan
   109→            else set()
   110→        )
   111→        for item in subjective_items:
   112→            if not scope_matches(item, resolved_options.scope):
   113→                continue
   114→            # Non-initial subjective items only surface when objective
   115→            # backlog is drained — unless the plan explicitly queued them
   116→            # (e.g. stale dims injected after a completed cycle).
   117→            item_id = item.get("id", "")
   118→            if not policy.should_surface(item) and item_id not in plan_queue_set:
   119→                continue
   120→            all_items.append(item)
   121→
   122→    # Inject triage stage items and workflow items when plan requires it
   123→    if resolved_options.plan and status in {"open", "all"}:
   124→        synth_items = build_triage_stage_items(resolved_options.plan, state)
   125→        all_items.extend(synth_items)
   126→        checkpoint_item = build_score_checkpoint_item(resolved_options.plan, state)
   127→        if checkpoint_item is not None:
   128→            all_items.append(checkpoint_item)
   129→        plan_item = build_create_plan_item(resolved_options.plan)
   130→        if plan_item is not None:
   131→            all_items.append(plan_item)
   132→
   133→    enrich_with_impact(all_items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
   134→
   135→    # Impact floor: drop mechanical findings with negligible score impact
   136→    MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT = 0.05
   137→    all_items = [
   138→        item for item in all_items
   139→        if item.get("kind") != "finding"
   140→        or item.get("is_review")
   141→        or item.get("is_subjective")
   142→        or not item.get("estimated_impact")
   143→        or item["estimated_impact"] >= MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT
   144→    ]
   145→
   146→    all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   147→
   148→    # Apply living plan ordering if provided
   149→    new_ids: set[str] = set()
   150→    if resolved_options.plan:
   151→        new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)
   152→        all_items = _apply_plan_order(
   153→            all_items,
   154→            resolved_options.plan,
   155→            include_skipped=resolved_options.include_skipped,
   156→            cluster=resolved_options.cluster,
   157→            new_ids=new_ids,
   158→        )
   159→
   160→    # Collapse auto-clusters into meta-items (unless drilling into a cluster)
   161→    should_collapse = (
   162→        resolved_options.collapse_clusters
   163→        and resolved_options.plan
   164→        and not resolved_options.cluster
   165→        and not resolved_options.plan.get("active_cluster")
   166→    )
   167→    if should_collapse:
   168→        all_items = _collapse_clusters(all_items, resolved_options.plan)
   169→
   170→    total = len(all_items)
   171→    if resolved_options.count is not None and resolved_options.count > 0:
   172→        all_items = all_items[: resolved_options.count]
   173→
   174→    if resolved_options.explain:
   175→        for item in all_items:
   176→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   177→
   178→    return {
   179→        "items": all_items,
   180→        "total": total,
   181→        "grouped": group_queue_items(all_items, "item"),
   182→        "new_ids": new_ids,
   183→    }
   184→
   185→
   186→__all__ = [
   187→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   188→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   189→    "WorkQueueResult",
   190→    "build_work_queue",
   191→    "group_queue_items",
   192→]
   193→
```
