1→"""Unified subjective-visibility policy.
     2→
     3→A single frozen dataclass computed once per operation replaces the scattered
     4→``has_objective_items`` / ``objective_count`` computations in
     5→``stale_dimensions``, ``auto_cluster``, and ``_work_queue/core``.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→# Detectors whose findings are NOT objective mechanical work.
    15→# Canonical definition — re-exported by stale_dimensions for back-compat.
    16→NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    17→    "review", "concerns", "subjective_review", "subjective_assessment",
    18→})
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    22→class SubjectiveVisibility:
    23→    """Immutable snapshot of the subjective-vs-objective balance."""
    24→
    25→    has_objective_backlog: bool  # any open non-subjective findings?
    26→    objective_count: int  # how many
    27→    unscored_ids: frozenset[str]  # subjective::* IDs needing initial review
    28→    stale_ids: frozenset[str]  # subjective::* IDs needing re-review
    29→    under_target_ids: frozenset[str]  # below target, not stale/unscored
    30→
    31→    def should_surface(self, item: dict) -> bool:
    32→        """Should this subjective queue item appear in the work queue?
    33→
    34→        Unscored (initial_review) -> always.  All others -> only when drained.
    35→        """
    36→        if item.get("initial_review"):
    37→            return True
    38→        return not self.has_objective_backlog
    39→
    40→    def should_inject_to_plan(self, fid: str) -> bool:
    41→        """Should this subjective ID be injected into plan queue_order?"""
    42→        if fid in self.unscored_ids:
    43→            return True  # unconditional
    44→        if fid in self.stale_ids:
    45→            return not self.has_objective_backlog
    46→        if fid in self.under_target_ids:
    47→            return not self.has_objective_backlog
    48→        return False
    49→
    50→    def should_evict_from_plan(self, fid: str) -> bool:
    51→        """Should this subjective ID be removed from plan queue_order?"""
    52→        if fid in self.unscored_ids:
    53→            return False  # never evict unscored
    54→        if fid in self.stale_ids or fid in self.under_target_ids:
    55→            return self.has_objective_backlog
    56→        return False
    57→
    58→    @property
    59→    def backlog_blocks_rerun(self) -> bool:
    60→        """Preflight: should reruns be blocked?"""
    61→        return self.has_objective_backlog
    62→
    63→
    64→def _is_evidence_only(finding: dict) -> bool:
    65→    """Return True if the finding is below its detector's standalone threshold."""
    66→    from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    67→    from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    68→
    69→    detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    70→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
    71→    if meta and meta.standalone_threshold:
    72→        threshold_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(meta.standalone_threshold, 9)
    73→        finding_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(finding.get("confidence", "low"), 9)
    74→        if finding_rank > threshold_rank:
    75→            return True
    76→    return False
    77→
    78→
    79→def compute_subjective_visibility(
    80→    state: StateModel,
    81→    *,
    82→    target_strict: float = 95.0,
    83→) -> SubjectiveVisibility:
    84→    """Build the policy snapshot from current state.
    85→
    86→    Imports building-block helpers from ``stale_dimensions`` so the
    87→    source-of-truth logic stays in one place.
    88→    """
    89→    from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    90→        _current_stale_ids,
    91→        current_under_target_ids,
    92→        current_unscored_ids,
    93→    )
    94→
    95→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    96→
    97→    # Count open, non-suppressed, objective findings.
    98→    # Evidence-only findings (below standalone confidence threshold) are
    99→    # excluded — they still affect scores but are not actionable queue items.
   100→    objective_count = sum(
   101→        1
   102→        for f in findings.values()
   103→        if f.get("status") == "open"
   104→        and f.get("detector") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   105→        and not f.get("suppressed")
   106→        and not _is_evidence_only(f)
   107→    )
   108→
   109→    unscored = current_unscored_ids(state)
   110→    stale = _current_stale_ids(state)
   111→    under_target = current_under_target_ids(state, target_strict=target_strict)
   112→
   113→    return SubjectiveVisibility(
   114→        has_objective_backlog=objective_count > 0,
   115→        objective_count=objective_count,
   116→        unscored_ids=frozenset(unscored),
   117→        stale_ids=frozenset(stale),
   118→        under_target_ids=frozenset(under_target),
   119→    )
   120→
   121→
   122→__all__ = [
   123→    "NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS",
   124→    "SubjectiveVisibility",
   125→    "compute_subjective_visibility",
   126→]
   127→
```

1→"""Tests for queue order guard in resolve command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→from unittest.mock import MagicMock
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.persistence import save_plan
    10→
    11→
    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Lazy import helper — avoids the circular import in resolve/render
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→
    16→def _import_guard():
    17→    """Import _check_queue_order_guard, patching away the render dependency."""
    18→    import desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd as cmd_mod
    19→    return cmd_mod._check_queue_order_guard
    20→
    21→
    22→def _get_guard_fn():
    23→    """Return the guard function, handling circular import gracefully."""
    24→    try:
    25→        return _import_guard()
    26→    except ImportError:
    27→        # Fall back: patch the problematic render import chain
    28→        import sys
    29→        stub = MagicMock()
    30→        modules_to_stub = [
    31→            "desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score_update",
    32→            "desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_helpers",
    33→        ]
    34→        saved = {}
    35→        for mod_name in modules_to_stub:
    36→            if mod_name in sys.modules:
    37→                saved[mod_name] = sys.modules[mod_name]
    38→            sys.modules[mod_name] = stub
    39→
    40→        # Force reimport
    41→        import desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd as cmd_mod
    42→        importlib.reload(cmd_mod)
    43→        fn = cmd_mod._check_queue_order_guard
    44→
    45→        # Restore
    46→        for mod_name in modules_to_stub:
    47→            if mod_name in saved:
    48→                sys.modules[mod_name] = saved[mod_name]
    49→            else:
    50→                sys.modules.pop(mod_name, None)
    51→        return fn
    52→
    53→
    54→_check_queue_order_guard = _get_guard_fn()
    55→

    77→    """Create and save a plan with given queue order, monkeypatch PLAN_FILE."""
    78→    import desloppify.engine._plan.persistence as persist_mod

    81→    plan = empty_plan()
    82→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)

    84→    if clusters:

1→"""Queue-order enforcement helpers for resolve command flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
     6→from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan
     7→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
     8→
     9→
    10→def _check_queue_order_guard(
    11→    state: dict,
    12→    patterns: list[str],
    13→    status: str,
    14→) -> bool:
    15→    """Warn and block if resolving items not at the front of the plan queue."""
    16→    if status != "fixed":
    17→        return False
    18→    try:
    19→        if not has_living_plan():
    20→            return False
    21→        plan = load_plan()
    22→        queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    23→        if not queue_order:
    24→            return False
    25→
    26→        result = build_work_queue(
    27→            state,
    28→            options=QueueBuildOptions(
    29→                plan=plan,
    30→                count=1,
    31→                collapse_clusters=True,
    32→                include_subjective=True,
    33→            ),
    34→        )
    35→        if not result["items"]:
    36→            return False
    37→
    38→        front_item = result["items"][0]
    39→        front_id = front_item["id"]
    40→
    41→        front_ids: set[str] = set()
    42→        if front_item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    43→            for member in front_item.get("members", []):
    44→                front_ids.add(member["id"])
    45→            front_ids.add(front_id)
    46→        else:
    47→            front_ids.add(front_id)
    48→
    49→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    50→        resolved_ids: set[str] = set()
    51→        for pattern in patterns:
    52→            if pattern in clusters:
    53→                resolved_ids.update(clusters[pattern].get("finding_ids", []))
    54→                resolved_ids.add(pattern)
    55→            else:
    56→                resolved_ids.add(pattern)
    57→
    58→        findings = state.get("findings", {})
    59→        resolved_ids = {
    60→            finding_id
    61→            for finding_id in resolved_ids
    62→            if finding_id in clusters
    63→            or (finding_id in findings and findings[finding_id].get("status") == "open")
    64→        }
    65→        if not resolved_ids:
    66→            return False
    67→
    68→        out_of_order = resolved_ids - front_ids
    69→        for cluster_id in list(out_of_order):
    70→            if cluster_id in clusters:
    71→                alive_members = {
    72→                    finding_id
    73→                    for finding_id in clusters[cluster_id].get("finding_ids", [])
    74→                    if finding_id in findings and findings[finding_id].get("status") == "open"
    75→                }
    76→                if alive_members and alive_members <= front_ids:
    77→                    out_of_order.discard(cluster_id)
    78→        if not out_of_order:
    79→            return False
    80→
    81→        print(colorize("\n  Queue order violation: these items are not next in the plan queue:\n", "yellow"))
    82→        for finding_id in sorted(out_of_order):
    83→            print(f"    {finding_id}")
    84→        print(colorize(f"\n  The current next item is: {front_id}", "dim"))
    85→        print(colorize("  Items must be resolved in plan order. If you need to reprioritize:", "dim"))
    86→        print(colorize("    desloppify plan move <pattern> --position top    # move to front", "dim"))
    87→        print(colorize("    desloppify plan skip <pattern> --reason '...'    # skip for now", "dim"))
    88→        print(colorize("    desloppify next                                  # see what's next\n", "dim"))
    89→        return True
    90→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
    91→        return False
    92→
    93→
    94→__all__ = ["_check_queue_order_guard"]
    95→
```

     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import scope_matches
     6→
     7→
     8→def _item(*, id: str, detector: str = "smells", file: str = "a.py") -> dict:

    10→        "id": id,
    11→        "detector": detector,

    19→class TestHashSuffixMatching:
    20→    def test_hash_suffix_matches(self):

    22→        assert scope_matches(item, "1497da0b") is True
    23→
    24→    def test_hash_suffix_no_match(self):
    25→        item = _item(id="smells::a.py::1497da0b")
    26→        assert scope_matches(item, "deadbeef") is False

    28→    def test_hash_suffix_case_insensitive(self):
    29→        item = _item(id="smells::a.py::1497da0b")
    30→        assert scope_matches(item, "1497DA0B") is True

    32→    def test_short_hex_not_treated_as_hash(self):

    38→    def test_detector_name_still_works(self):
    39→        item = _item(id="smells::a.py::abcdef01", detector="smells")
    40→        assert scope_matches(item, "smells") is True

---

## Work Queue Simplification Plan

### Problem Summary

The work queue system has filtering logic scattered across 3 files (`ranking.py`, `core.py`, `plan_order.py`), ordering that happens in two incompatible passes (`item_sort_key` then `apply_plan_order`), and enrichment in 4 separate places. A developer debugging "why does item X appear/not appear in the queue" must trace through 5 files and 30+ functions.

The goal: someone investigating a bug should be able to answer the question by reading ONE function in ONE file.

---

### Phase 1: Inline `apply_plan_order` into `build_work_queue` (decompose the reordering pass)

**What changes**: The 120-line `apply_plan_order()` function does four distinct things that are currently tangled:
1. Enrichment (stamping `plan_description`, `plan_note`, `plan_cluster` onto items)
2. Reordering (queue_order first, then existing, then new)
3. Filtering (skipped items, cluster focus)
4. Metadata stamping (`queue_position`, `plan_skipped`, etc.)

This phase decomposes these into named steps visible inside `build_work_queue`, making the pipeline legible from one place.

**Files modified**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` -- main pipeline
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py` -- break into smaller exported functions

**Conceptual before**:
```python
# core.py line 152
all_items = _apply_plan_order(all_items, plan, include_skipped=..., cluster=..., new_ids=...)
```

**Conceptual after**:
```python
# core.py -- each step is visible and named
_enrich_plan_metadata(all_items, plan)  # stamps plan_description, plan_note, plan_cluster
all_items = _plan_sort(all_items, plan, new_ids=new_ids)  # ordered + existing + new partition
all_items = _filter_skipped(all_items, plan, include_skipped=include_skipped)
all_items = _filter_cluster_focus(all_items, plan, cluster=cluster)
_stamp_queue_positions(all_items, plan)  # queue_position, plan_skipped, plan_skip_kind
```

Each of these sub-functions lives in `plan_order.py` and is individually testable, but the pipeline reads top-to-bottom in `core.py`.

**Key design decisions**:
- `_enrich_plan_metadata` mutates items in-place (already the pattern for `enrich_with_impact`)
- `_plan_sort` returns a new list (same as current)
- `_filter_skipped` and `_filter_cluster_focus` return new lists
- `_stamp_queue_positions` mutates in-place
- The old `apply_plan_order` function becomes a thin wrapper that calls these in sequence, preserved for backward compatibility if any external code uses it directly

**Verification**: Run the full test suite (`python -m pytest desloppify/tests/ -q`). The 42 tests in `work_queue_cases.py`, 41 in `test_auto_cluster.py`, and 12 in `test_queue_order_guard.py` all exercise the exact output of `build_work_queue` with plans. If any ordering or filtering changes, these tests break.

**Risk**: Low. This is purely structural decomposition. The before/after output of `build_work_queue` is identical.

**Lines changed**: ~80 in `plan_order.py` (break apart), ~30 in `core.py` (replace single call with named steps).

---

### Phase 2: Unify the sort key (merge plan-order into `item_sort_key`)

**What changes**: Currently `item_sort_key()` sorts by impact/confidence/tier, then `apply_plan_order` undoes that ordering for planned items. This means the sort key lies -- it does not describe the actual ordering. This phase makes the sort key truthful by incorporating plan position as the primary sort component.

**Files modified**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` -- extend `item_sort_key`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` -- remove the plan-reordering step, pass plan context to sort

**Conceptual before (two passes)**:
```python
all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)            # pass 1: natural order
all_items = _plan_sort(all_items, plan, ...)  # pass 2: undo pass 1 for planned items
```

**Conceptual after (one pass)**:
```python
sort_ctx = PlanSortContext(plan, new_ids) if plan else None
all_items.sort(key=lambda item: item_sort_key(item, plan_ctx=sort_ctx))
```

Where the extended `item_sort_key` returns:
- `(0, queue_order_position, ...)` for items in `queue_order`
- `(1, *natural_key)` for existing items not in queue_order
- `(2, *natural_key)` for new items (appeared this scan)
- When `plan_ctx is None`: returns `(1, *natural_key)` for everything (preserves planless behavior)

**Key design decisions**:
- `PlanSortContext` is a small frozen dataclass with the precomputed `queue_order` position map and `new_ids` set. It is constructed once before sorting.
- Workflow stages still get their blocked-before-unblocked re-sort. This can be handled by `item_sort_key` directly: `(0, queue_position, blocked_penalty, stage_index)`.
- The `_plan_sort` function from Phase 1 is removed entirely.

**Verification**: Same test suite. Additionally, write a focused test that constructs items with a plan and verifies that `sorted(items, key=item_sort_key)` produces the same order as the current two-pass approach.

**Risk**: Medium. The two-pass approach has subtle interactions (workflow stage re-sorting happens after the partition). Need to verify the triage-stage ordering test in `test_epic_triage.py` still passes.

**Lines changed**: ~40 in `ranking.py` (extend sort key), ~-30 in `core.py` (remove plan sort step).

---

### Phase 3: Consolidate filtering into a single predicate

**What changes**: Currently, items are filtered in 3 separate locations:
1. `build_finding_items()` in `ranking.py`: suppressed, status, chronic, evidence-only threshold, scope
2. `build_work_queue()` in `core.py`: subjective visibility policy (via `should_surface`), impact floor (0.05)
3. `apply_plan_order()` in `plan_order.py`: skipped items, cluster focus

After Phase 1 decomposes these, we consolidate the remaining non-plan filters. The goal: a single function `should_include_item()` that answers "does this item appear in the queue?" with one call.

**Files modified**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` -- new predicate, simplify pipeline
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` -- move impact floor into `build_finding_items` or into predicate
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` -- potentially extract predicate here

**Conceptual before**:
```python
# ranking.py: build_finding_items filters suppressed, status, chronic, evidence-only, scope
# core.py: filters by subjective policy + plan_queue_set override
# core.py: filters by impact floor
# plan_order.py: filters by skipped, cluster focus
```

**Conceptual after**:
```python
# core.py: pipeline with visible filter steps
all_items = _collect_all_candidates(state, options)  # no filtering except suppressed/status
all_items = _apply_filters(all_items, options, policy, plan_queue_set)
# Where _apply_filters checks: scope, evidence-only, subjective policy, impact floor
# Plan-level filters (skipped, cluster) remain separate (after sort) since they depend on ordering
```

The key insight is that `_apply_filters` is the ONE FUNCTION to read when debugging "why is item X missing."

**Key design decisions**:
- Keep `_collect_all_candidates` doing minimal work: just finding-to-item conversion (status and suppressed are not really "filters" -- they're just not candidates at all)
- `_apply_filters` is a list comprehension with a readable predicate that short-circuits
- The impact floor moves from a post-enrichment filter into the predicate (it checks `estimated_impact`)
- Plan-level filters (skipped, cluster focus) stay as separate named steps because they depend on plan ordering context

**Verification**: Same test suite. Key tests: `test_low_confidence_props_excluded_from_queue`, `test_impact_floor_filters_negligible_impact`, `test_subjective_items_do_not_starve_objective_queue_head`.

**Risk**: Medium. The impact floor currently runs after `enrich_with_impact`, and the evidence-only filter runs inside `build_finding_items` before enrichment. These have different timing in the pipeline. The predicate needs to respect this: evidence-only is a candidate-level filter (doesn't need impact), but impact-floor needs the `estimated_impact` stamp.

**Solution**: Keep the two-stage structure but make it explicit:
```python
all_items = _collect_all_candidates(state, options)  # evidence-only filtered here
enrich_with_impact(all_items, dimension_scores)
all_items = [item for item in all_items if _passes_post_enrich_filters(item, options, policy)]
```

**Lines changed**: ~50 in `core.py` (new predicate functions), ~-20 in `ranking.py` (simplify build_finding_items).

---

### Phase 4: Extract synthetic item builders from `helpers.py`

**What changes**: `helpers.py` is a 462-line grab bag. It contains two unrelated categories:
1. **Predicates and utilities** (60 lines): `scope_matches`, `status_matches`, `is_subjective_finding`, `detail_dict`, `slugify`, etc.
2. **Synthetic item builders** (200 lines): `build_subjective_items`, `build_triage_stage_items`, `build_score_checkpoint_item`, `build_create_plan_item`
3. **Scoring helpers** (100 lines): `subjective_strict_scores`, related dimension alias machinery

These should be split into focused modules.

**Files modified**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` -- keep predicates/utilities only
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py` -- new file for synthetic builders
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` -- update imports
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/work_queue.py` -- facade re-exports unchanged

**Conceptual before**:
```
helpers.py (462 lines) -- predicates, scoring, synthetic builders all mixed
```

**Conceptual after**:
```
helpers.py (~160 lines) -- predicates, utilities, scoring
synthetic.py (~300 lines) -- build_subjective_items, build_triage_stage_items, etc.
```

**Key design decisions**:
- The facade (`work_queue.py`) re-exports stay the same. No external API change.
- `build_subjective_items` depends on `subjective_strict_scores` and the alias machinery. These move together into `synthetic.py`.
- `detail_dict`, `scope_matches`, `status_matches`, `slugify` stay in `helpers.py` since they're used everywhere.
- `is_review_finding`, `is_subjective_finding`, `review_finding_weight` stay in `helpers.py` (used by ranking.py).

**Verification**: Same test suite. Also check that `from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import build_triage_stage_items` in `test_epic_triage.py` is updated to use the new path (or the old path still works via re-export).

**Risk**: Low. This is a file split with no behavioral change. The only risk is broken imports in test files that import directly from `_work_queue.helpers`.

**Lines changed**: ~-300 in `helpers.py`, ~+300 in new `synthetic.py`, ~10 import updates.

---

### Phase 5: Document the pipeline with a docstring roadmap

**What changes**: After phases 1-4, `build_work_queue` should read as a clear pipeline. This phase adds a comprehensive docstring to `build_work_queue` that documents the pipeline stages and the filtering contract.

**Files modified**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` -- docstring

**Conceptual after**:
```python
def build_work_queue(state, *, options=None):
    """Build a ranked, filtered work queue.

    Pipeline stages:
    1. COLLECT — gather finding items + synthetic items (subjective, triage, workflow)
       Filters applied: suppressed, status, chronic, evidence-only threshold
    2. GATE — apply subjective visibility policy (plan queue_order overrides)
    3. ENRICH — stamp estimated_impact from dimension scores
    4. FILTER — impact floor (mechanical findings with impact < 0.05 dropped)
    5. SORT — single sort by item_sort_key (plan position > natural ranking)
    6. PLAN METADATA — stamp plan_description, plan_note, plan_cluster
    7. PLAN FILTER — remove skipped items, filter to cluster focus
    8. DISPLAY — collapse clusters, apply count limit, stamp explain
    
    To debug "why is item X missing?": check stages 1, 2, 4, 7 in order.
    To debug "why is item X in wrong position?": check stage 5.
    """
```

**Risk**: Zero. Documentation only.

---

### Phase Ordering and Dependencies

```
Phase 1 (decompose apply_plan_order)
  |
  v
Phase 2 (unify sort key) -- depends on Phase 1 decomposition
  |
  v
Phase 3 (consolidate filtering) -- can proceed independently from Phase 2
  |
  v
Phase 4 (split helpers.py) -- fully independent, can be done at any point
  |
  v
Phase 5 (documentation) -- should come last
```

Phases 1 and 4 are the safest starting points. Phase 2 is the highest-value change (removes the "sort then unsort" antipattern) but carries the most risk.

---

### Recommended Implementation Order

1. **Phase 4** first (lowest risk, immediate readability win for helpers.py)
2. **Phase 1** next (structural decomposition, makes pipeline visible)
3. **Phase 5** after Phase 1 (document the now-visible pipeline)
4. **Phase 3** (consolidate filters into predicates)
5. **Phase 2** last (highest value but highest risk, save for when pipeline is clean)
